Create `internal/staff/team.go`:

```go
package staff

import (
	"context"
	"errors"
	"time"

	"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5"

	"github.com/aleogr/marketplace/internal/identity"
	"github.com/aleogr/marketplace/internal/platform/audit"
)

var (
	// ErrOwnAccount is a staff member resetting their own second factor, or
	// deactivating themselves: their recovery codes and the security page
	// are for the one, and the other would lock them out mid-change.
	ErrOwnAccount = errors.New("staff: nobody does this to their own account")
	// ErrOwnerProtected is a change nobody makes to the owner: deactivating
	// them, or resetting their second factor, which only the owner changes
	// (D9).
	ErrOwnerProtected = errors.New("staff: the owner is not deactivated, and only the owner changes the owner's second factors")
)

// Colleague is a staff member as the staff list shows them: who they are,
// whether they are the owner or deactivated, the kinds of second factor they
// have, and the roles they hold where.
type Colleague struct {
	ID            string
	Name          string
	Email         string
	Owner         bool
	DeactivatedAt *time.Time
	Factors       []identity.Method
	Roles         []Held
}

// Held is a role a staff member holds, in a scope: one marketplace, or the
// whole platform (Marketplace is Platform).
type Held struct {
	Role            string
	Name            string
	Owner           bool
	Marketplace     string
	MarketplaceName string
}

// Team is the platform's staff as the console manages them (F15 spec, D5,
// D9): the list, the invitations, a colleague's second factor reset and a
// staff member deactivated. Every change is made by a staff member whose
// roles allow it across the platform, in one transaction with its audit
// record, the staff member as its actor.
type Team struct {
	db       Transactor
	identity *identity.Service
	audit    Auditor
	now      func() time.Time
}

// NewTeam returns the staff, read and written through db and the identity
// flows of service, audited to auditor.
func NewTeam(db Transactor, service *identity.Service, auditor Auditor) *Team {
	return &Team{db: db, identity: service, audit: auditor, now: func() time.Time { return time.Now().UTC() }}
}

// List returns every staff member: those who are not deactivated first,
// each group by name.
func (t *Team) List(ctx context.Context) ([]Colleague, error) {
	var colleagues []Colleague
	err := t.db.InTx(ctx, func(tx pgx.Tx) error {
		var err error
		colleagues, err = readColleagues(ctx, tx, "")
		return err
	})
	return colleagues, err
}

// Colleague returns one staff member, or ErrNotStaff.
func (t *Team) Colleague(ctx context.Context, id string) (Colleague, error) {
	if !validID(id) {
		return Colleague{}, ErrNotStaff
	}
	var colleagues []Colleague
	err := t.db.InTx(ctx, func(tx pgx.Tx) error {
		var err error
		colleagues, err = readColleagues(ctx, tx, id)
		return err
	})
	if err != nil {
		return Colleague{}, err
	}
	if len(colleagues) == 0 {
		return Colleague{}, ErrNotStaff
	}
	return colleagues[0], nil
}

// readColleagues reads the staff member id, or every staff member when id
// is "". The platform's transaction sees staff accounts and their second
// factors, and nobody else's (D4).
func readColleagues(ctx context.Context, tx pgx.Tx, id string) ([]Colleague, error) {
	rows, err := tx.Query(ctx, `
		SELECT a.id::text, a.name, a.email, a.deactivated_at,
		       EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM user_role u WHERE u.account_id = a.id AND u.owner),
		       coalesce(array_agg(DISTINCT f.kind) FILTER (WHERE f.kind IS NOT NULL), '{}')
		  FROM account a LEFT JOIN second_factor f ON f.account_id = a.id
		 WHERE a.kind = 'staff' AND ($1 = '' OR a.id = nullif($1, '')::uuid)
		 GROUP BY a.id
		 ORDER BY a.deactivated_at IS NOT NULL, lower(a.name), a.id`, id)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	colleagues, err := pgx.CollectRows(rows, func(row pgx.CollectableRow) (Colleague, error) {
		var c Colleague
		var kinds []string
		err := row.Scan(&c.ID, &c.Name, &c.Email, &c.DeactivatedAt, &c.Owner, &kinds)
		for _, kind := range kinds {
			c.Factors = append(c.Factors, identity.Method(kind))
		}
		return c, err
	})
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	held, err := readHeld(ctx, tx, id)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	for i := range colleagues {
		colleagues[i].Roles = held[colleagues[i].ID]
	}
	return colleagues, nil
}

// readHeld reads the roles the staff member id holds, or every staff
// member's when id is "", by account: the owner role first, then by name.
func readHeld(ctx context.Context, tx pgx.Tx, id string) (map[string][]Held, error) {
	rows, err := tx.Query(ctx, `
		SELECT u.account_id::text, r.id::text, r.name, r.owner,
		       coalesce(u.marketplace_id::text, ''), coalesce(m.name, '')
		  FROM user_role u
		  JOIN role r ON r.id = u.role_id
		  LEFT JOIN (SELECT DISTINCT marketplace_id, name FROM tenancy_host_map()) m
		         ON m.marketplace_id = u.marketplace_id::text
		 WHERE $1 = '' OR u.account_id = nullif($1, '')::uuid
		 ORDER BY r.owner DESC, lower(r.name), r.id, u.marketplace_id NULLS FIRST`, id)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	defer rows.Close()
	held := map[string][]Held{}
	for rows.Next() {
		var account string
		var h Held
		if err := rows.Scan(&account, &h.Role, &h.Name, &h.Owner, &h.Marketplace, &h.MarketplaceName); err != nil {
			return nil, err
		}
		held[account] = append(held[account], h)
	}
	return held, rows.Err()
}

// ResetFactor resets the second factor of the staff member account, as
// actor, who may reset factors across the platform: every method, recovery
// code and session of theirs goes, and they enrol a new factor at their next
// sign-in (identity.Service.ResetFactors). Nobody resets their own, and
// nobody resets the owner's (D9).
func (t *Team) ResetFactor(ctx context.Context, actor Actor, v identity.Visit, account string) error {
	var factors []string
	change := t.change(actor, StaffResetFactor, func(ctx context.Context, tx pgx.Tx, target identity.Account) error {
		return tx.QueryRow(ctx, `
			SELECT coalesce(array_agg(kind ORDER BY kind), '{}') FROM second_factor WHERE account_id = $1`,
			target.ID).Scan(&factors)
	})
	change.Then = func(ctx context.Context, tx pgx.Tx, target identity.Account) error {
		return record(ctx, tx, t.audit, actor, "staff.factor_reset", personal(target.ID),
			described{"factors": factors}, described{})
	}
	return t.identity.ResetFactors(ctx, v, account, change)
}

// Deactivate deactivates the staff member account, as actor, who may
// deactivate staff across the platform: they sign in no more, their sessions
// end and their roles are removed (identity.Service.Deactivate). Nobody
// deactivates themselves, and nobody deactivates the owner (D9).
func (t *Team) Deactivate(ctx context.Context, actor Actor, v identity.Visit, account string) error {
	var roles []described
	change := t.change(actor, StaffDeactivate, func(ctx context.Context, tx pgx.Tx, target identity.Account) error {
		held, err := readHeld(ctx, tx, target.ID)
		for _, h := range held[target.ID] {
			roles = append(roles, described{"role": h.Role, "scope": scopeName(h.Marketplace)})
		}
		return err
	})
	change.Then = func(ctx context.Context, tx pgx.Tx, target identity.Account) error {
		// The owner's assignment is refused by the database too
		// (migrations/00019_roles.sql).
		if _, err := tx.Exec(ctx, `DELETE FROM user_role WHERE account_id = $1`, target.ID); err != nil {
			return err
		}
		return record(ctx, tx, t.audit, actor, "staff.deactivated", personal(target.ID),
			described{"roles": roles}, described{})
	}
	return t.identity.Deactivate(ctx, v, account, change)
}

// change is a colleague's change to a staff account, as actor, who must be
// allowed permission across the platform: never to their own account, never
// to the owner's; read is what the change reads of the account before it is
// made, for its audit record.
func (t *Team) change(actor Actor, permission Permission, read func(ctx context.Context, tx pgx.Tx, target identity.Account) error) identity.StaffChange {
	return identity.StaffChange{By: actor.Name, Check: func(ctx context.Context, tx pgx.Tx, target identity.Account) error {
		if !validID(actor.Account) {
			return ErrNotAllowed
		}
		allowed, err := allows(ctx, tx, actor.Account, permission, Platform)
		if err != nil {
			return err
		}
		if !allowed {
			return ErrNotAllowed
		}
		if target.ID == actor.Account {
			return ErrOwnAccount
		}
		owner, err := isOwner(ctx, tx, target.ID)
		if err != nil {
			return err
		}
		if owner {
			return ErrOwnerProtected
		}
		return read(ctx, tx, target)
	}}
}

// isOwner reports whether account holds the owner role.
func isOwner(ctx context.Context, tx pgx.Tx, account string) (bool, error) {
	var owner bool
	err := tx.QueryRow(ctx, `SELECT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM user_role WHERE account_id = $1 AND owner)`, account).Scan(&owner)
	return owner, err
}

// personal is a staff account as an audit record's subject: the record is
// about them, so what it says is sealed with their key.
func personal(account string) audit.Subject {
	return audit.Subject{Kind: "account", ID: account, Person: account}
}
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Commit**

```bash
git add -A docs/infrastructure.md internal/identity internal/platform/mail internal/staff migrations web/mail
git commit -F - <<'EOF'
A colleague's second factor reset and a staff member deactivated, the owner kept by the database

Staff recovery and offboarding (F15 spec, D9), at the service. A reset
takes every app, key and recovery code of a colleague, ends every session
and every sign-in waiting for its second step, and tells them by e-mail who
did it; their next sign-in, with the password, opens a session with no
second factor, which the console sends to enrol a new one. A deactivation
marks the account, ends its sessions and removes its roles; a sign-in with
it is answered as a wrong password is, after the same work, and recorded as
refused with the reason; its sessions open nothing even if one survived.

The identity service makes the change to the account's own rows, in the
order every other path takes them, and runs the console's check and record
in the same transaction: only a staff member allowed across the platform
changes a colleague, nobody changes their own account this way, and nobody
deactivates the owner or resets the owner's factor. The database refuses the
owner's again, through a trigger on the two columns that record a
deactivation and a reset, and the owner's assignment is already kept, so a
mistake in the code cannot lock the owner out.

The staff list's reading is here too: each member with their roles where,
the kinds of second factor they have and whether they are the owner or
deactivated. The notices exist in both languages, text and HTML.
EOF
```


## Task 15: A reset's second factor comes back through a mailed link, never through the password alone

D9, the owner's decision of 2026-09-26: a reset mails the colleague a link that works once,
for 24 hours, and only that link, with the password too, opens a session that may enrol a new app or key.
A newer reset replaces an older link; an expired, used or replaced link, or a deactivated colleague's, is
"no longer valid" (410) and its page says whom to ask. A staff account with no second factor is never
signed in by its password alone: after the right password `SignIn` refuses it with `ErrEnrolByLink`, and
the console says the one sentence that sends the person to the mail — the same whether or not a live link
exists. The first factor is added only in a session that proved more than the password: the first run's,
an invitation's (PR 3's next tasks) and a reset link's. The console tests that relied on a factor-less
sign-in now use the session their creation opened. The owner's manual recovery in
`docs/infrastructure.md` writes a reset link by hand, and a test runs its statements as written.

**Files:**
- Create: `migrations/00022_factor_reset_link.sql`, `web/newfactor.go`, `web/newfactor.templ`, `internal/platform/httpx/newfactor.go`, `internal/platform/httpx/newfactor_integration_test.go`
- Modify: `migrations/00021_staff_offboarding.sql`, `internal/identity/identity.go`, `internal/identity/staff.go`, `internal/identity/staff_integration_test.go`, `internal/staff/team_integration_test.go`, `internal/platform/mail/templates_test.go`, `web/mail/second-factor-reset.en-US.html`, `web/mail/second-factor-reset.en-US.txt`, `web/mail/second-factor-reset.pt-BR.html`, `web/mail/second-factor-reset.pt-BR.txt`, `web/locales/en-US.json`, `web/locales/pt-BR.json`, `internal/platform/httpx/console.go`, `internal/platform/httpx/console_integration_test.go`, `internal/platform/httpx/console_test.go`, `internal/platform/httpx/identity.go`, `internal/platform/httpx/roles_integration_test.go`, `docs/infrastructure.md`
- Regenerated by `make generate` (committed): the `*_templ.go` of every template this task touches

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `ResetFactors`, `changeStaff`, `deactivatedRows` (task 14); `identity.NewToken`, `Visit.link`,
  `lockAccount`, `passwordStill`, `insertSession`, `openSession`; the sign-in limit `id.Limits.SignIn`.
- Produces: migration `00022_factor_reset_link.sql` (`factor_reset_link`, platform-only RLS);
  `identity.NewFactorPath = "/new-factor"`, `identity.FactorResetLinkLifetime = 24 * time.Hour`,
  `identity.ErrEnrolByLink`; `func (s *Service) FactorResetLink(ctx context.Context, v Visit, token string) (Account, error)`;
  `func (s *Service) UseFactorResetLink(ctx context.Context, v Visit, token, password string) (string, error)`
  (audits `identity.factor_reset_link_used`); `SignIn` audits `identity.signin_failed` `{reason: no_factor}`;
  `internal/platform/httpx/newfactor.go` (`GET|POST /new-factor`); `web.NewFactor`, templ `NewFactorPage`;
  locale keys `console.signin.no_factor`, `console.new_factor.*`; the `second-factor-reset` mail with
  `{{.Link}}` and `{{.Hours}}`; test helpers `linkToken`, `storedLinkHash`, `sessionsOf`, `console.ownerSession`,
  `(c console) inSession(t, token)`, `resetLink(t, to)`, `resetOnConsole(t, account)`, `recoveryStatements(t)`.

```go
// A reset's link works for a day, and a newer reset replaces it: an expired
// link and a replaced one are no longer valid, as an unknown one is, and a
// deactivated colleague's opens nothing either. Nothing is recorded as used.
func TestAResetLinkWorksOnceForADayAndANewerResetReplacesIt(t *testing.T) {
	s, db, _, _, trail := service(t)
	email, token := staffMember(t, s)
	session, err := s.AuthenticateStaff(t.Context(), token)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	reset := func() string {
		t.Helper()
		if err := s.ResetFactors(t.Context(), platformVisit(), session.Account.ID, StaffChange{By: "Dona"}); err != nil {
			t.Fatalf("ResetFactors = %v", err)
		}
		return linkToken(t, noticeTo(t, db, "second-factor-reset", email)["Link"])
	}
	first := reset()
	second := reset()
	if first == second {
		t.Fatal("two resets mailed the same link")
	}
	for name, link := range map[string]string{"replaced": first, "unknown": "no such link"} {
		if _, err := s.FactorResetLink(t.Context(), platformVisit(), link); !errors.Is(err, ErrTokenInvalid) {
			t.Errorf("reading a %s link = %v, want ErrTokenInvalid", name, err)
		}
		if _, err := s.UseFactorResetLink(t.Context(), platformVisit(), link, "correct horse battery staple"); !errors.Is(err, ErrTokenInvalid) {
			t.Errorf("using a %s link = %v, want ErrTokenInvalid", name, err)
		}
	}

	realNow := s.now
	s.now = func() time.Time { return realNow().Add(FactorResetLinkLifetime + time.Minute) }
	if _, err := s.FactorResetLink(t.Context(), platformVisit(), second); !errors.Is(err, ErrTokenInvalid) {
		t.Fatalf("reading an expired link = %v, want ErrTokenInvalid", err)
	}
	if _, err := s.UseFactorResetLink(t.Context(), platformVisit(), second, "correct horse battery staple"); !errors.Is(err, ErrTokenInvalid) {
		t.Fatalf("using an expired link = %v, want ErrTokenInvalid", err)
	}
	s.now = realNow

	live := reset()
	if err := s.Deactivate(t.Context(), platformVisit(), session.Account.ID, StaffChange{By: "Dona"}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Deactivate = %v", err)
	}
	if _, err := s.UseFactorResetLink(t.Context(), platformVisit(), live, "correct horse battery staple"); !errors.Is(err, ErrTokenInvalid) {
		t.Fatalf("a deactivated colleague's link = %v, want ErrTokenInvalid", err)
	}
	if storedLinkHash(t, db, session.Account.ID) != nil {
		t.Fatal("the deactivation left the colleague's link behind")
	}
	if slices.Contains(trail.actions(), "identity.factor_reset_link_used") {
		t.Fatal("a link that opened nothing was recorded as used")
	}
}

// A staff account with no second factor is never signed in by its password
// alone, whoever made it so: the first run's, an invitation's or a reset
// link's session is the only way to its first factor (F15 spec, D9). The
// refusal comes after the password, so it tells nobody without the password
// anything; its record says why. A buyer with none signs in as before.
func TestThePasswordAloneNeverLeadsAStaffMemberToEnrol(t *testing.T) {
	s, db, one, _, trail := service(t)
	email, token := staffMember(t, s)
	session, err := s.AuthenticateStaff(t.Context(), token)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if _, err := s.SignIn(t.Context(), platformVisit(), email, "correct horse battery staple"); !errors.Is(err, ErrEnrolByLink) {
		t.Fatalf("SignIn with no factor = %v, want ErrEnrolByLink", err)
	}
	if _, err := s.SignIn(t.Context(), platformVisit(), email, "not the password at all"); !errors.Is(err, ErrCredentials) {
		t.Fatalf("a wrong password with no factor = %v, want ErrCredentials", err)
	}
	refused := 0
	for _, e := range trail.entries {
		if e.Action == "identity.signin_failed" && e.Subject.ID == session.Account.ID && strings.Contains(string(e.After), `"reason":"no_factor"`) {
			refused++
		}
	}
	if refused != 1 {
		t.Fatalf("%d refusals recorded the missing factor as their reason, want the right password's one", refused)
	}
	if n := sessionsOf(t, db, session.Account.ID); n != 1 {
		t.Fatalf("the account has %d sessions, want only the one its creation opened", n)
	}

	confirmed(t, s, db, one, "buyer-without-factor@example.test")
	if token := signIn(t, s, one, "buyer-without-factor@example.test", "correct horse battery staple"); token == "" {
		t.Fatal("a buyer with no second factor was not signed in")
	}
}

// linkToken is the token a mailed link carries.
func linkToken(t *testing.T, link string) string {
	t.Helper()
	parsed, err := url.Parse(link)
	if err != nil || parsed.Query().Get("token") == "" {
		t.Fatalf("the link %q carries no token: %v", link, err)
	}
	return parsed.Query().Get("token")
}

// storedLinkHash is what the database keeps of account's reset link, nil
// when it has none.
func storedLinkHash(t *testing.T, db serving, account string) []byte {
	t.Helper()
	var hash []byte
	if err := db.InTx(t.Context(), func(tx pgx.Tx) error {
		err := tx.QueryRow(t.Context(), `SELECT token_hash FROM factor_reset_link WHERE account_id = $1`, account).Scan(&hash)
		if errors.Is(err, pgx.ErrNoRows) {
			return nil
		}
		return err
	}); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	return hash
}

// sessionsOf counts account's sessions, live or not.
func sessionsOf(t *testing.T, db serving, account string) int {
	t.Helper()
	var n int
	if err := db.InTx(t.Context(), func(tx pgx.Tx) error {
		return tx.QueryRow(t.Context(), `SELECT count(*) FROM session WHERE account_id = $1`, account).Scan(&n)
	}); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	return n
}
```

// The owner's manual recovery, run as docs/infrastructure.md writes it, by
// someone with the migrator's access: every factor and session of the
// owner goes, and a reset's link written by hand is then the owner's way to
// a new factor, since the password alone opens nothing (D9). The statements
// are read from the guide itself, so the guide cannot drift from what works.
func TestTheOwnersManualRecoveryLeadsToANewFactorThroughALink(t *testing.T) {
	tm := newTeam(t)
	tm.withFactor(t, tm.owner)
	const handMade = "0f1e2d3c4b5a69788796a5b4c3d2e1f00f1e2d3c4b5a69788796a5b4c3d2e1f0"

	for _, statement := range recoveryStatements(t) {
		var args []any
		if strings.Contains(statement, ":'owner'") {
			args = append(args, tm.owner)
		}
		if strings.Contains(statement, ":'token'") {
			args = append(args, handMade)
		}
		statement = strings.NewReplacer(":'owner'", fmt.Sprintf("$%d", 1),
			":'token'", fmt.Sprintf("$%d", len(args))).Replace(statement)
		if _, err := tm.database.pool.Exec(t.Context(), statement, args...); err != nil {
			t.Fatalf("the guide's statement %q: %v", statement, err)
		}
	}
	if n := tm.count(t, `SELECT count(*) FROM second_factor WHERE account_id = $1`, tm.owner); n != 0 {
		t.Fatalf("the recovery left the owner %d factors", n)
	}
	if _, err := tm.signIn(t, "owner@example.test"); !errors.Is(err, identity.ErrEnrolByLink) {
		t.Fatalf("the owner's password alone after the recovery = %v, want ErrEnrolByLink", err)
	}
	session, err := tm.service.UseFactorResetLink(t.Context(), platform(), handMade, password)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("the hand-made link = %v", err)
	}
	if owner, err := tm.service.AuthenticateStaff(t.Context(), session); err != nil || owner.Account.ID != tm.owner || owner.SecondFactor {
		t.Fatalf("the hand-made link opened %+v, %v; want the owner's session with no factor", owner, err)
	}
	tm.allows(t, tm.owner, staff.RolesManage, staff.Platform, true)
}

// recoveryStatements are the statements of the owner's manual recovery in
// docs/infrastructure.md that change something, in order, as psql would run
// them with :owner and :token set.
func recoveryStatements(t *testing.T) []string {
	t.Helper()
	guide, err := os.ReadFile("../../docs/infrastructure.md")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	_, section, found := strings.Cut(string(guide), "### If the owner loses every second factor and every recovery code")
	_, block, opened := strings.Cut(section, "```sql\n")
	block, _, closed := strings.Cut(block, "```")
	if !found || !opened || !closed {
		t.Fatal("the guide has no recovery procedure to run")
	}
	var statements []string
	for _, statement := range strings.Split(block, ";") {
		var kept []string
		for _, line := range strings.Split(statement, "\n") {
			if !strings.HasPrefix(strings.TrimSpace(line), "--") {
				kept = append(kept, line)
			}
		}
		statement = strings.TrimSpace(strings.Join(kept, "\n"))
		switch {
		case statement == "", statement == "BEGIN", statement == "COMMIT", strings.HasPrefix(statement, "SELECT"):
			continue
		}
		statements = append(statements, statement)
	}
	if len(statements) < 7 {
		t.Fatalf("the guide's recovery reads as %d statements: %q", len(statements), statements)
	}
	return statements
}

```go
// The reset's notice carries the link that is the only way to a new second
// factor, says it works once for a day, and says the password alone will not
// do (F15 spec, D9, the owner's decision of 2026-09-26).
func TestTheResetCarriesItsLinkAndItsLifetime(t *testing.T) {
	for _, tc := range []struct{ language, hours, alone string }{
		{"en-US", "works once, for 24 hours", "Signing in with your password alone will not"},
		{"pt-BR", "funciona uma vez, por 24 horas", "Entrar só com a senha não"},
	} {
		rendered, err := templates(t).Render(mail.Message{
			Template: "second-factor-reset", Language: tc.language, To: "reader@example.test", From: "Console",
			Variables: map[string]string{"Name": "Leitora", "By": "Dona", "Hours": "24",
				"Link": "https://console.example/" + tc.language + "/new-factor?token=sample"},
		}, i18n.Default)
		if err != nil {
			t.Fatalf("second-factor-reset in %s: %v", tc.language, err)
		}
		for _, part := range []string{rendered.Text, rendered.HTML} {
			for _, want := range []string{"/new-factor?token=sample", tc.hours, tc.alone} {
				if !strings.Contains(part, want) {
					t.Errorf("second-factor-reset in %s does not say %q: %s", tc.language, want, part)
				}
			}
		}
		if !strings.Contains(rendered.HTML, `<a href="https://console.example/`+tc.language+`/new-factor?token=sample">`) {
			t.Errorf("second-factor-reset in %s: the link is not a link: %s", tc.language, rendered.HTML)
		}
	}
}
```

Create `internal/platform/httpx/newfactor_integration_test.go`:

```go
//go:build integration

package httpx_test

import (
	"net/http"
	"net/http/httptest"
	"net/url"
	"strings"
	"testing"

	"github.com/aleogr/marketplace/internal/identity"
)

// resetLink is the path and query of the link the last reset of to's second
// factor mailed, read from the outbox before it is dispatched.
func (c console) resetLink(t *testing.T, to string) string {
	t.Helper()
	var link string
	if err := c.pool.QueryRow(t.Context(), `
		SELECT payload->'Variables'->>'Link' FROM outbox_event
		 WHERE kind = 'email.send' AND payload->>'Template' = 'second-factor-reset' AND payload->>'To' = $1
		 ORDER BY created_at DESC, id DESC LIMIT 1`, to).Scan(&link); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("no reset link was mailed to %s: %v", to, err)
	}
	parsed, err := url.Parse(link)
	if err != nil || !strings.HasPrefix(parsed.Path, "/pt-BR"+identity.NewFactorPath) || parsed.Query().Get("token") == "" {
		t.Fatalf("the reset mailed %q", link)
	}
	return strings.TrimPrefix(parsed.Path, "/pt-BR") + "?" + parsed.RawQuery
}

// resetOnConsole resets account's second factor as the console does, on a
// platform visit that builds the console's links.
func (c console) resetOnConsole(t *testing.T, account string) {
	t.Helper()
	if err := c.service.ResetFactors(t.Context(), identity.Visit{Platform: true, Language: "pt-BR",
		MarketplaceName: "Console", BaseURL: "https://console.test"}, account, identity.StaffChange{By: "Dona"}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("ResetFactors = %v", err)
	}
}

// After a reset, the colleague's password alone opens nothing: the sign-in
// says to use the mailed link. The link's page names them, and with their
// password opens a session that goes to add a new app or key; then it is no
// longer valid, as an unknown link is (F15 spec, D9, the owner's decision
// of 2026-09-26).
func TestAResetColleagueAddsANewFactorThroughTheMailedLinkOnly(t *testing.T) {
	c := newConsole(t, true)
	colleague, account, _ := c.colleague(t, "colega@example.test")
	c.resetOnConsole(t, account)

	wantRedirect(t, colleague.get("/account/security"), "/pt-BR/signin")
	refused := colleague.signIn("colega@example.test", ownerPassword)
	if refused.Code != http.StatusUnauthorized || !strings.Contains(refused.Body.String(), "Use o link do e-mail") ||
		!strings.Contains(refused.Body.String(), `role="alert"`) {
		t.Fatalf("the password alone after a reset: status %d, body %s", refused.Code, refused.Body.String())
	}
	if wrong := colleague.signIn("colega@example.test", "not the password at all"); wrong.Code != http.StatusUnauthorized ||
		strings.Contains(wrong.Body.String(), "Use o link do e-mail") {
		t.Fatalf("a wrong password after a reset: status %d, body %s", wrong.Code, wrong.Body.String())
	}

	link := c.resetLink(t, "colega@example.test")
	token := strings.TrimPrefix(link, identity.NewFactorPath+"?token=")
	page := colleague.get(link)
	body := page.Body.String()
	if page.Code != http.StatusOK || !strings.Contains(body, "Colega") ||
		!strings.Contains(body, `action="/pt-BR/new-factor"`) || !strings.Contains(body, `value="colega@example.test"`) ||
		!strings.Contains(body, `autocomplete="current-password"`) || !strings.Contains(body, `name="query" value="token=`+token+`"`) {
		t.Fatalf("the link's page: status %d, body %s", page.Code, body)
	}

	form := func(password string) url.Values { return url.Values{"token": {token}, "password": {password}} }
	wrong := colleague.post(identity.NewFactorPath, form("not the password at all"))
	if wrong.Code != http.StatusUnauthorized || !strings.Contains(wrong.Body.String(), "A senha não confere.") ||
		!strings.Contains(wrong.Body.String(), `aria-invalid="true"`) {
		t.Fatalf("the link with a wrong password: status %d, body %s", wrong.Code, wrong.Body.String())
	}
	wantRedirect(t, colleague.post(identity.NewFactorPath, form(ownerPassword)), "/pt-BR/enrol")
	wantRedirect(t, colleague.get("/account/security"), "/pt-BR/enrol")
	enrolAppOnConsole(t, colleague)
	if security := colleague.get("/account/security"); security.Code != http.StatusOK {
		t.Fatalf("the colleague's security page with the new app = %d", security.Code)
	}

	for what, answer := range map[string]*httptest.ResponseRecorder{
		"the link again":        c.onConsole(t).get(link),
		"the link posted again": c.onConsole(t).post(identity.NewFactorPath, form(ownerPassword)),
		"an unknown link":       c.onConsole(t).get(identity.NewFactorPath + "?token=no-such-link"),
	} {
		if gone := answer.Body.String(); answer.Code != http.StatusGone || !strings.Contains(gone, "não é mais válido") ||
			strings.Contains(gone, `name="password"`) {
			t.Errorf("%s: status %d, body %s", what, answer.Code, gone)
		}
	}
}
```

Create `migrations/00022_factor_reset_link.sql`:

```sql
-- The link a colleague's reset of a staff member's second factor mails them
-- (docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-26-f15-console-design.md, D9; the owner's
-- decision of 2026-09-26).
--
-- A reset takes every app, key and recovery code of the account. The new
-- factor is added only through this link, with the password too: a sign-in
-- with the password alone never leads to enrolment, so a password that
-- leaked is not enough to put somebody else's factor on the account. The
-- link works once, for 24 hours; a newer reset replaces it, which is why
-- the account is the key: one link per account at most, the last one.
--
-- The token is kept as its SHA-256 only, as an invitation's and a
-- confirmation link's are. `used_at` records the use, which the audit log
-- records too (`identity.factor_reset_link_used`).
--
-- The table belongs to the platform: staff are its only accounts, and only
-- a transaction that names no marketplace reads or writes it.

-- +goose Up
CREATE TABLE factor_reset_link (
    account_id uuid        PRIMARY KEY REFERENCES account (id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    token_hash bytea       NOT NULL UNIQUE,
    created_at timestamptz NOT NULL,
    expires_at timestamptz NOT NULL,
    used_at    timestamptz,
    CONSTRAINT factor_reset_link_expires_after_it_is_made CHECK (expires_at > created_at)
);

ALTER TABLE factor_reset_link ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;

CREATE POLICY factor_reset_link_belongs_to_the_platform ON factor_reset_link
    FOR ALL USING (current_marketplace_id() IS NULL)
    WITH CHECK (current_marketplace_id() IS NULL);

-- +goose Down
DROP TABLE IF EXISTS factor_reset_link;
```

```go
}

// ResetFactors removes every second factor of the staff account target, its
// recovery codes and its enrolments in progress, ends its sign-ins waiting
// for a second step and every session it has, and tells its owner by
// e-mail. The next sign-in, with the password, opens a session with no
// second factor, which the console sends to enrol a new one before anything
// else (F15 spec, D9; D4). The account's row records when, which the
// database refuses for the owner (migrations/00021_staff_offboarding.sql).
//
// The rows go in the order every path takes them — challenges, factors,
// sessions, the audit chain, the failure count last — so a reset never
// waits on a sign-in that waits on it.
func (s *Service) ResetFactors(ctx context.Context, v Visit, target string, change StaffChange) error {
	return s.changeStaff(ctx, v, target, change, "second-factor-reset", func(tx pgx.Tx, account string, now time.Time) error {
		if _, err := tx.Exec(ctx, `UPDATE account SET factor_reset_at = $2 WHERE id = $1`, account, now); err != nil {
			return err
```

with:

```go
}

// NewFactorPath is where a reset's link leads, on the console's host: the
// only way a staff member whose second factor was reset adds a new one.
const NewFactorPath = "/new-factor"

// FactorResetLinkLifetime is how long a reset's link works (the owner's
// decision of 2026-09-26).
const FactorResetLinkLifetime = 24 * time.Hour

// ErrEnrolByLink is a right password on a staff account with no second
// factor. The password alone never leads to enrolment (F15 spec, D9): a
// staff member adds their first factor in the session the first run, their
// invitation or a reset's link opened, and nowhere else. As ErrUnverified,
// it says more than "refused" only to whoever has the password.
var ErrEnrolByLink = errors.New("identity: a staff account with no second factor adds one through its link")

// ResetFactors removes every second factor of the staff account target, its
// recovery codes and its enrolments in progress, ends its sign-ins waiting
// for a second step and every session it has, and mails its owner a link,
// which works once for FactorResetLinkLifetime and replaces any earlier one.
// The link, with the password, is the only way to a new factor: the
// password alone opens nothing while the account has none (F15 spec, D9;
// the owner's decision of 2026-09-26). The account's row records when, which
// the database refuses for the owner (migrations/00021_staff_offboarding.sql).
//
// The rows go in the order every path takes them — challenges, factors,
// sessions, the link, the audit chain, the failure count last — so a reset
// never waits on a sign-in that waits on it.
func (s *Service) ResetFactors(ctx context.Context, v Visit, target string, change StaffChange) error {
	token, hash, err := NewToken()
	if err != nil {
		return err
	}
	link := map[string]string{
		"Link":  v.link(NewFactorPath, url.Values{"token": {token}}),
		"Hours": strconv.Itoa(int(FactorResetLinkLifetime / time.Hour)),
	}
	return s.changeStaff(ctx, v, target, change, "second-factor-reset", link, func(tx pgx.Tx, account string, now time.Time) error {
		if _, err := tx.Exec(ctx, `UPDATE account SET factor_reset_at = $2 WHERE id = $1`, account, now); err != nil {
			return err
```

```go
// putResetLink stores the SHA-256 of a reset's link for account, in place of
// the one an earlier reset left, used or not.
func putResetLink(ctx context.Context, tx pgx.Tx, account string, hash []byte, now time.Time) error {
	_, err := tx.Exec(ctx, `
		INSERT INTO factor_reset_link (account_id, token_hash, created_at, expires_at)
		VALUES ($1, $2, $3, $4)
		ON CONFLICT (account_id) DO UPDATE
		   SET token_hash = EXCLUDED.token_hash, created_at = EXCLUDED.created_at,
		       expires_at = EXCLUDED.expires_at, used_at = NULL`,
		account, hash, now, now.Add(FactorResetLinkLifetime))
	return err
}

// accountByResetLink reads the active staff account whose reset link hashes
// to hash, while the link is unused and unexpired at now, or pgx.ErrNoRows.
func accountByResetLink(ctx context.Context, tx pgx.Tx, hash []byte, now time.Time) (Account, error) {
	return scanAccount(tx.QueryRow(ctx, `
		SELECT `+accountColumns+` FROM account
		 WHERE kind = 'staff' AND deactivated_at IS NULL
		   AND id = (SELECT account_id FROM factor_reset_link
		              WHERE token_hash = $1 AND used_at IS NULL AND expires_at > $2)`, hash, now))
}

// FactorResetLink reads whose a reset's link is, for its page, or answers
// ErrTokenInvalid for a link that is unknown, used, replaced or expired, or
// a deactivated colleague's: which one it was is nobody's business.
func (s *Service) FactorResetLink(ctx context.Context, v Visit, token string) (Account, error) {
	if !v.Platform {
		return Account{}, errVisitScope
	}
	var account Account
	err := s.within(ctx, v, func(tx pgx.Tx) error {
		found, err := accountByResetLink(ctx, tx, HashToken(token), s.now())
		if errors.Is(err, pgx.ErrNoRows) {
			return ErrTokenInvalid
		}
		account = found
		return err
	})
	return account, err
}

// UseFactorResetLink checks a reset's link and the account's password, and
// opens a session with no second factor, which the console sends to add a
// new one before anything else (F15 spec, D9). The link is then used, and
// its use audited with the staff member as its actor. A wrong password is
// refused as a sign-in's is, and leaves the link as it was.
//
// As SignIn does, it reads in one transaction, verifies the password with
// none open, and writes in a second, which checks again that the link is
// still unused and the password still the one verified.
func (s *Service) UseFactorResetLink(ctx context.Context, v Visit, token, password string) (string, error) {
	if !v.Platform {
		return "", errVisitScope
	}
	if len(password) > maxPasswordBytes {
		return "", ErrCredentials
	}
	hash := HashToken(token)
	var account Account
	var secret string
	err := s.within(ctx, v, func(tx pgx.Tx) error {
		found, err := accountByResetLink(ctx, tx, hash, s.now())
		if errors.Is(err, pgx.ErrNoRows) {
			return ErrTokenInvalid
		}
		if err != nil {
			return err
		}
		account = found
		secret, err = passwordOf(ctx, tx, found.ID)
		return err
	})
	if err != nil {
		return "", err
	}
	ok, _, err := s.hasher.Verify(ctx, password, secret)
	if err != nil {
		return "", err
	}
	if !ok {
		if err := s.within(ctx, v, func(tx pgx.Tx) error {
			return s.refusalWith(ctx, tx, v, account.ID, "identity.signin_failed", map[string]string{"reason": "factor_reset_link"})
		}); err != nil {
			return "", err
		}
		return "", ErrCredentials
	}

	session, sessionHash, err := NewToken()
	if err != nil {
		return "", err
	}
	err = s.within(ctx, v, func(tx pgx.Tx) error {
		if err := lockAccount(ctx, tx, account.ID); err != nil {
			return err
		}
		now := s.now()
		current, err := accountByResetLink(ctx, tx, hash, now)
		if errors.Is(err, pgx.ErrNoRows) || (err == nil && current.ID != account.ID) {
			// Used, replaced or deactivated since the first read.
			return ErrTokenInvalid
		}
		if err != nil {
			return err
		}
		if still, err := passwordStill(ctx, tx, account.ID, secret); err != nil || !still {
			if err == nil {
				err = ErrCredentials
			}
			return err
		}
		if factors, err := countFactors(ctx, tx, account.ID); err != nil || factors > 0 {
			if err == nil {
				// A link opens a session that has proved no second factor:
				// never on an account that has one.
				s.log.WarnContext(ctx, "a reset's link was refused: the account has a second factor", "account", account.ID)
				err = ErrTokenInvalid
			}
			return err
		}
		if _, err := tx.Exec(ctx, `UPDATE factor_reset_link SET used_at = $2 WHERE account_id = $1`, account.ID, now); err != nil {
			return err
		}
		if err := s.record(ctx, tx, v, account.ID, "identity.factor_reset_link_used"); err != nil {
			return err
		}
		if err := insertSession(ctx, tx, "", account.ID, sessionHash, v.IP, v.UserAgent, now); err != nil {
			return err
		}
		return s.record(ctx, tx, v, account.ID, "identity.signin")
	})
	if err != nil {
		return "", err
	}
	return session, nil
}

```

Create `web/newfactor.go`:

```go
package web

// NewFactor is the page a reset's link opens: whose account it is, the
// token the form sends back, and the password's form; Invalid when the link
// is no longer valid (F15 spec, D9).
type NewFactor struct {
	Name    string
	Email   string
	Token   string
	Invalid bool
	Form    Form
}
```

Create `web/newfactor.templ`:

```templ
package web

import "strconv"

// NewFactorPage is where a reset's link leads: the staff member gives their
// password and goes on to add a new app or key; or reads that the link is no
// longer valid, and whom to ask for another (F15 spec, D9).
templ NewFactorPage(page Page, view NewFactor) {
	@Layout(page, page.T("console.new_factor.title")) {
		<h1>{ page.T("console.new_factor.title") }</h1>
		if view.Invalid {
			<p id={ ErrorID } role="alert">{ page.T("console.new_factor.invalid") }</p>
		} else {
			<p>{ page.T("console.new_factor.intro", view.Name) }</p>
			@refusal(view.Form, page)
			<form method="post" action={ templ.SafeURL("/" + page.Language + "/new-factor") }>
				<input type="hidden" name="csrf_token" value={ page.CSRFToken }/>
				<input type="hidden" name="token" value={ view.Token }/>
				<input type="hidden" name="username" autocomplete="username" value={ view.Email }/>
				<label>
					{ page.T("identity.field.password") }
					<input
						name="password"
						type="password"
						autocomplete="current-password"
						required
						maxlength={ strconv.Itoa(view.Form.MaxLength) }
						{ view.Form.Input("password")... }
					/>
				</label>
				<button type="submit">{ page.T("console.new_factor.submit") }</button>
			</form>
		}
	}
}
```

Create `internal/platform/httpx/newfactor.go`:

```go
package httpx

import (
	"errors"
	"net/http"
	"net/url"

	"github.com/aleogr/marketplace/internal/identity"
	"github.com/aleogr/marketplace/internal/platform/i18n"
	"github.com/aleogr/marketplace/web"
)

// A reset's link (F15 spec, D9; the owner's decision of 2026-09-26): the
// only way a staff member whose second factor was reset adds a new one. The
// link and the password together open a session with no second factor,
// which the console sends to add an app or a key before anything else; the
// password alone never does.

// showNewFactor renders the link's page with status.
func (s Site) showNewFactor(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request, status int, view web.NewFactor) {
	page := s.page(r, identity.NewFactorPath)
	if view.Token != "" {
		// The page is the link's, token and all, whichever language it is
		// switched to.
		page.Query = url.Values{"token": {view.Token}}.Encode()
	}
	renderStatus(w, r, status, web.NewFactorPage(page, view))
}

// newFactorView reads whose link token is, and says it is no longer valid
// when it is not: unknown, used, replaced or expired alike, answered 410.
func (s Site) newFactorView(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request, token string, form web.Form) (web.NewFactor, bool) {
	account, err := s.identity.Service.FactorResetLink(r.Context(), visit(r), token)
	if errors.Is(err, identity.ErrTokenInvalid) {
		s.showNewFactor(w, r, http.StatusGone, web.NewFactor{Invalid: true})
		return web.NewFactor{}, false
	}
	if err != nil {
		s.failed(w, r, "a reset's link could not be read", err)
		return web.NewFactor{}, false
	}
	return web.NewFactor{Name: account.Name, Email: account.Email, Token: token, Form: form}, true
}

func (s Site) newFactorPage(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
	if view, ok := s.newFactorView(w, r, r.URL.Query().Get("token"), newForm()); ok {
		s.showNewFactor(w, r, http.StatusOK, view)
	}
}

// useNewFactorLink checks the link and the password, and signs the staff
// member in to add an app or a key before anything else (D4, D9). A wrong
// password is told so on its field, as a refused sign-in is, and the link
// still works.
func (s Site) useNewFactorLink(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
	token := r.PostFormValue("token")
	session, err := s.identity.Service.UseFactorResetLink(r.Context(), visit(r), token, r.PostFormValue("password"))
	switch {
	case errors.Is(err, identity.ErrTokenInvalid):
		s.showNewFactor(w, r, http.StatusGone, web.NewFactor{Invalid: true})
	case errors.Is(err, identity.ErrCredentials):
		form := newForm()
		form.Error, form.Field = "console.new_factor.wrong_password", "password"
		if view, ok := s.newFactorView(w, r, token, form); ok {
			s.showNewFactor(w, r, http.StatusUnauthorized, view)
		}
	case err != nil:
		s.failed(w, r, "a reset's link could not be used", err)
	default:
		s.openSession(w, r, session)
		http.Redirect(w, r, "/"+i18n.FromContext(r.Context())+enrolPath, http.StatusSeeOther)
	}
}
```

with:

```markdown

(`\set owner '<the id>'` and `\set token '<a token>'` in `psql` first, the
token made with `openssl rand -hex 32` and given to the owner by hand, never
written down anywhere else.) The owner then opens
`https://console.marketplace.lab.aleogr.dev/en-US/new-factor?token=<the token>`,
gives the password, and is sent to enrol a new app or key, with new recovery
codes, before anything else. Signing in with the password alone would not do:
a staff account with no second factor is never signed in that way, the
owner's included (F15 spec, D9). The same procedure brings back an owner who
left the first run before adding a factor. The migration user owns the
tables, so row-level security does not hide the owner's rows from it. The
statements above are run as written here, against a real database, by
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Commit**

```bash
git add -A docs/infrastructure.md internal/identity internal/platform/httpx internal/platform/mail internal/staff migrations web
git commit -F - <<'EOF'
A reset's second factor comes back through a mailed link, never through the password alone

The owner's decision of 2026-09-26 on the factor reset (F15 spec, D9). A
reset used to let the colleague enrol a new factor at their next sign-in
with the password alone, so whoever knew the password - a leaked one - could
sign in first and put their own factor on the account. Now a reset mails the
colleague a link that works once, for 24 hours, and only that link, with the
password too, opens a session that may enrol a new app or key. A newer reset
replaces an older link; an expired, used or replaced link, or a deactivated
colleague's, is no longer valid, and its page says whom to ask for a new
reset. The token is kept as its SHA-256, and its use is audited with the
colleague as the actor.

A staff account with no second factor is never signed in by its password
alone: after the right password the sign-in is refused, as an unconfirmed
address is, with the one sentence that tells the person to use the link in
the e-mail or ask for a new reset - the same whether or not a live link
exists, since the password's holder is owed the remedy and nothing more.
The refusal is recorded with its reason. The first factor is added only in
a session that proved more than the password: the first run's, which the
owner holds the bootstrap token for, an invitation's and a reset link's. The
console tests that relied on a factor-less sign-in now use the session
their creation opened, as the first run and an invitation do; the owner's
manual recovery in docs/infrastructure.md writes a reset link by hand, and
serves an owner who left the first run before enrolling too.

The reset's notice becomes the mail carrying the link and its lifetime, in
both languages, text and HTML; the link's page and the sign-in's refusal
exist in both languages too.
EOF
```


## Task 16: The staff list: each member's roles and second factor, a factor reset and a deactivation, confirmed

The spec's flow "Staff list": every staff member with the roles they hold and where, the kinds
of second factor they have, the owner and the reader marked, a deactivated member marked apart. D6 has no
permission of its own for reading the list, so it opens to whoever holds any of the three `staff.*`
permissions across the platform, and is "not found" to anybody else. Each member offers the changes the
reader may make, on whoever they can be made to, each confirmed on a page of its own after the step-up
(D10); a change nobody may make — to one's own account, to the owner, to a deactivated member — is said on
that page (409), before any step-up, and not offered.

Create `internal/platform/httpx/staff_integration_test.go`:

```go
//go:build integration

package httpx_test

import (
	"net/http"
	"net/url"
	"strings"
	"testing"

	"github.com/aleogr/marketplace/internal/identity"
	"github.com/aleogr/marketplace/internal/platform/audit"
	"github.com/aleogr/marketplace/internal/staff"
)

// The staff list shows every member with the roles they hold where and
// their second factor, marks the owner and the reader, and offers each
// change the reader's roles allow on whoever it can be made to: the owner
// sees both changes on a colleague and none on themselves; a colleague who
// may only reset factors sees that alone; one who may do neither finds no
// list at all (D8, D9).
func TestTheStaffListShowsRolesAndSecondFactors(t *testing.T) {
	c := newConsole(t, true)
	owner, _ := c.signedInOwner(t)
	_, account, _ := c.colleague(t, "colega@example.test")
	c.grant(t, account, c.marketplace.ID, staff.ConsoleView)

	list := owner.get("/staff")
	body := list.Body.String()
	for _, want := range []string{
		`href="/pt-BR/staff" aria-current="page"`, `<span class="protected">Proprietário</span>`,
		`<span class="badge">Você</span>`, "colega@example.test", "Role 1 — em: Loja", "Aplicativo autenticador",
		`href="/pt-BR/staff/` + account + `/reset"`, `href="/pt-BR/staff/` + account + `/deactivate"`,
	} {
		if !strings.Contains(body, want) {
			t.Fatalf("the staff list (status %d) does not hold %q: %s", list.Code, want, body)
		}
	}
	if strings.Contains(body, `href="/pt-BR/staff/`+c.ownerID(t)+`/`) {
		t.Fatalf("the staff list offers a change to the owner: %s", body)
	}

	resetter, resetterAccount, _ := c.colleague(t, "resetter@example.test")
	c.grant(t, resetterAccount, staff.Platform, staff.ConsoleView, staff.StaffResetFactor)
	seen := resetter.get("/staff")
	if seen.Code != http.StatusOK || !strings.Contains(seen.Body.String(), `href="/pt-BR/staff/`+account+`/reset"`) ||
		strings.Contains(seen.Body.String(), "/deactivate") || !strings.Contains(seen.Body.String(), `href="/pt-BR/staff"`) {
		t.Fatalf("the staff list for who may only reset: status %d, body %s", seen.Code, seen.Body.String())
	}

	viewer, viewerAccount, _ := c.colleague(t, "viewer@example.test")
	c.grant(t, viewerAccount, staff.Platform, staff.ConsoleView)
	if home := viewer.get("/"); strings.Contains(home.Body.String(), `href="/pt-BR/staff"`) {
		t.Fatalf("the menu offers the staff to who may not see them: %s", home.Body.String())
	}
	for _, path := range []string{"/staff", "/staff/" + account + "/reset", "/staff/" + account + "/deactivate"} {
		if answer := viewer.get(path); answer.Code != http.StatusNotFound {
			t.Errorf("%s for who may not see the staff = %d, want 404", path, answer.Code)
		}
	}
	for _, path := range []string{"/staff/" + account + "/deactivate", "/staff/" + account + "/reset"} {
		if answer := viewer.post(path, nil); answer.Code != http.StatusNotFound {
			t.Errorf("POST %s for who may not see the staff = %d, want 404", path, answer.Code)
		}
	}
	if answer := resetter.get("/staff/" + account + "/deactivate"); answer.Code != http.StatusNotFound {
		t.Errorf("the deactivation for who may only reset = %d, want 404", answer.Code)
	}
}

// Resetting a colleague's second factor from the console: confirmed on its
// own page after the second factor is asked again, it ends the colleague's
// session; their password alone then opens nothing, and the mailed link
// sends them to enrol a new factor; it is audited with the owner as the
// actor. Nobody resets their own this way (D9, D10).
func TestAColleaguesFactorIsResetFromTheConsole(t *testing.T) {
	c := newConsole(t, true)
	owner, key := c.signedInOwner(t)
	colleague, account, _ := c.colleague(t, "colega@example.test")
	path := "/staff/" + account + "/reset"

	wantRedirect(t, owner.get(path), "/pt-BR/account/verify?for=staff&next=%2Fstaff%2F"+account+"%2Freset")
	stepUpOnConsole(t, owner, key, path)
	wantStatus(t, owner.get(path), http.StatusOK, "Colega perde todos os aplicativos autenticadores")
	wantRedirect(t, owner.post(path, nil), "/pt-BR/staff?done=reset")
	wantStatus(t, owner.get("/staff?done=reset"), http.StatusOK, "O segundo fator foi redefinido.")
	if entry, ok := c.trail.find("staff.factor_reset"); !ok || entry.Actor != (audit.Actor{ID: c.ownerID(t), Kind: audit.Staff}) ||
		entry.Subject.ID != account || entry.From != "203.0.113.20" {
		t.Fatalf("staff.factor_reset = %+v (found %v)", entry, ok)
	}

	wantRedirect(t, colleague.get("/"), "/pt-BR/signin")
	wantStatus(t, colleague.signIn("colega@example.test", ownerPassword), http.StatusUnauthorized, "Use o link do e-mail")
	token := strings.TrimPrefix(c.resetLink(t, "colega@example.test"), identity.NewFactorPath+"?token=")
	wantRedirect(t, colleague.post(identity.NewFactorPath, url.Values{"token": {token}, "password": {ownerPassword}}), "/pt-BR/enrol")
	wantRedirect(t, colleague.get("/"), "/pt-BR/enrol")

	own := owner.get("/staff/" + c.ownerID(t) + "/reset")
	wantStatus(t, own, http.StatusConflict, "Ninguém faz isso com a própria conta aqui")
	if strings.Contains(own.Body.String(), `method="post" action="/pt-BR/staff/`) {
		t.Fatalf("the refusal offers the change anyway: %s", own.Body.String())
	}
	wantStatus(t, owner.post("/staff/"+c.ownerID(t)+"/reset", nil), http.StatusConflict, "Ninguém faz isso com a própria conta aqui")
	if answer := owner.get("/staff/00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000009/reset"); answer.Code != http.StatusNotFound {
		t.Fatalf("the reset of nobody = %d, want 404", answer.Code)
	}
}

// Deactivating a staff member from the console: they are signed out, and
// their password is then answered as a wrong one is; the list shows them
// deactivated and offers nothing more. Whoever may deactivate staff cannot
// deactivate the owner, by the page or by a post (D9).
func TestAStaffMemberIsDeactivatedFromTheConsole(t *testing.T) {
	c := newConsole(t, true)
	colleague, account, _ := c.colleague(t, "colega@example.test")
	manager, managerAccount, key := c.colleague(t, "gestora@example.test")
	c.grant(t, managerAccount, staff.Platform, staff.ConsoleView, staff.StaffDeactivate)
	path := "/staff/" + account + "/deactivate"

	stepUpOnConsole(t, manager, key, path)
	wantStatus(t, manager.get(path), http.StatusOK, "Colega não poderá mais entrar")
	wantRedirect(t, manager.post(path, nil), "/pt-BR/staff?done=deactivated")
	list := manager.get("/staff?done=deactivated")
	wantStatus(t, list, http.StatusOK, `<span class="badge deactivated">Desativado</span>`)
	if strings.Contains(list.Body.String(), path) {
		t.Fatalf("the list offers a change to a deactivated member: %s", list.Body.String())
	}
	if entry, ok := c.trail.find("staff.deactivated"); !ok || entry.Actor.ID != managerAccount || entry.Subject.ID != account {
		t.Fatalf("staff.deactivated = %+v (found %v)", entry, ok)
	}

	wantRedirect(t, colleague.get("/"), "/pt-BR/signin")
	wantStatus(t, colleague.signIn("colega@example.test", ownerPassword), http.StatusUnauthorized, "O e-mail ou a senha não conferem.")
	wantStatus(t, manager.get(path), http.StatusConflict, "Este membro da equipe está desativado.")

	ownerPath := "/staff/" + c.ownerID(t) + "/deactivate"
	wantStatus(t, manager.get(ownerPath), http.StatusConflict, "O proprietário nunca é desativado")
	wantStatus(t, manager.post(ownerPath, url.Values{}), http.StatusConflict, "O proprietário nunca é desativado")
	c.signedInOwner(t)
	wantRedirect(t, c.onConsole(t).signIn(ownerEmail, ownerPassword), "/pt-BR/signin/verify")
}
```

Create `web/staff.go`:

```go
package web

// The staff pages of the console (F15 spec, Flows "Staff list"). A staff
// member's name and address, a role's name and a marketplace's name are
// people's words, shown as they were typed; everything else goes through a
// key.

// Colleague is a staff member as the staff list shows them. Factors are the
// keys of the labels of the kinds of second factor they have. Self is the
// staff member reading the page; Reset and Deactivate are the actions the
// reader may take on them.
type Colleague struct {
	ID          string
	Name        string
	Email       string
	Owner       bool
	Self        bool
	Deactivated bool
	Factors     []string
	Roles       []HeldRole
	Reset       bool
	Deactivate  bool
}

// HeldRole is a role a staff member holds: its name, or the owner role's,
// and where — Where is the marketplace's name, or empty for the whole
// platform, and Scope its id.
type HeldRole struct {
	Name  string
	Owner bool
	Scope string
	Where string
}

// StaffList is the staff, and what was just done.
type StaffList struct {
	Members []Colleague
	Done    string
}

// StaffAction is the confirmation of a change to one staff member: Action
// is "reset" or "deactivate", and Form the refusal when the change cannot
// be made, in which case the page offers none.
type StaffAction struct {
	Member Colleague
	Action string
	Form   Form
}

// HeldName is what a held role is called on a page.
func HeldName(page Page, role HeldRole) string {
	if role.Owner {
		return page.T(OwnerName)
	}
	return role.Name
}

// HeldWhere is where a role is held, as a page says it.
func HeldWhere(page Page, role HeldRole) string {
	return Where(page, Holder{Scope: role.Scope, Where: role.Where})
}

// ActionKey is the key of a staff action's text: its title, its
// explanation, its button.
func ActionKey(action, part string) string {
	return "console.staff." + action + "." + part
}
```

Create `web/staff.templ`:

```templ
package web

// StaffList is every staff member, with the roles they hold where and the
// state of their second factor, and the changes the reader may make to each
// (F15 spec, Flows "Staff list").
templ StaffPage(page Page, view StaffList) {
	@Layout(page, page.T("console.staff.title")) {
		<h1>{ page.T("console.staff.title") }</h1>
		if view.Done != "" {
			<p role="status">{ page.T(view.Done) }</p>
		}
		<p>{ page.T("console.staff.intro") }</p>
		<ul class="staff">
			for _, member := range view.Members {
				<li>
					<h2>
						{ member.Name }
						if member.Owner {
							<span class="protected">{ page.T(OwnerName) }</span>
						}
						if member.Self {
							<span class="badge">{ page.T("console.staff.you") }</span>
						}
						if member.Deactivated {
							<span class="badge deactivated">{ page.T("console.staff.deactivated") }</span>
						}
					</h2>
					<p>{ member.Email }</p>
					<dl>
						<dt>{ page.T("console.staff.roles") }</dt>
						if len(member.Roles) == 0 {
							<dd>{ page.T("console.staff.no_roles") }</dd>
						}
						for _, role := range member.Roles {
							<dd>{ page.T("console.staff.role_in", HeldName(page, role), HeldWhere(page, role)) }</dd>
						}
						<dt>{ page.T("console.staff.factors") }</dt>
						if len(member.Factors) == 0 {
							<dd>{ page.T("console.staff.no_factor") }</dd>
						}
						for _, factor := range member.Factors {
							<dd>{ page.T(factor) }</dd>
						}
					</dl>
					if member.Reset || member.Deactivate {
						<p class="actions">
							if member.Reset {
								<a
									href={ templ.SafeURL("/" + page.Language + "/staff/" + member.ID + "/reset") }
									aria-label={ page.T("console.staff.reset_named", member.Name) }
								>{ page.T("console.staff.reset.submit") }</a>
							}
							if member.Deactivate {
								<a
									href={ templ.SafeURL("/" + page.Language + "/staff/" + member.ID + "/deactivate") }
									aria-label={ page.T("console.staff.deactivate_named", member.Name) }
								>{ page.T("console.staff.deactivate.submit") }</a>
							}
						</p>
					}
				</li>
			}
		</ul>
	}
}

// StaffActionPage asks to confirm a change to one staff member — resetting
// their second factor, or deactivating them — saying what it does; or says
// why it cannot be made, and offers nothing (F15 spec, D9).
templ StaffActionPage(page Page, view StaffAction) {
	@Layout(page, page.T(ActionKey(view.Action, "title"), view.Member.Name)) {
		<h1>{ page.T(ActionKey(view.Action, "title"), view.Member.Name) }</h1>
		if view.Form.Error != "" {
			<p id={ ErrorID } role="alert">{ page.T(view.Form.Error) }</p>
		} else {
			<p>{ page.T(ActionKey(view.Action, "intro"), view.Member.Name) }</p>
			<form method="post" action={ templ.SafeURL("/" + page.Language + "/staff/" + view.Member.ID + "/" + view.Action) }>
				<input type="hidden" name="csrf_token" value={ page.CSRFToken }/>
				<button type="submit">{ page.T(ActionKey(view.Action, "submit")) }</button>
			</form>
		}
		<p><a href={ templ.SafeURL("/" + page.Language + "/staff") }>{ page.T("console.staff.back") }</a></p>
	}
}
```

Create `internal/platform/httpx/staff.go`:

```go
package httpx

import (
	"errors"
	"net/http"
	"slices"

	"github.com/aleogr/marketplace/internal/identity"
	"github.com/aleogr/marketplace/internal/platform/i18n"
	"github.com/aleogr/marketplace/internal/staff"
	"github.com/aleogr/marketplace/web"
)

// The staff pages of the console (F15 spec, Flows "Staff list"): every staff
// member with their roles and the state of their second factor, and the
// changes the reader's roles allow — resetting a colleague's second factor,
// deactivating a staff member — each confirmed on a page of its own and
// asked for the second factor again first (D9, D10). Each page is "not
// found" to whoever may not open it (D8).

// staffPath is the staff list; a member's changes are below it, by id.
const staffPath = "/staff"

// staffPermissions are what opens the staff list: any of them, across the
// platform. Each change asks for its own.
var staffPermissions = []staff.Permission{staff.StaffInvite, staff.StaffDeactivate, staff.StaffResetFactor}

// staffActions are the changes to one staff member, by the path that names
// each, with the permission it asks for.
var staffActions = map[string]staff.Permission{
	"reset":      staff.StaffResetFactor,
	"deactivate": staff.StaffDeactivate,
}

// staffDone is what a redirect back to the staff list may say was done.
var staffDone = map[string]string{
	"reset":       "console.staff.done.reset",
	"deactivated": "console.staff.done.deactivated",
}

// staffRoutes mounts the staff pages, behind secured and their permissions.
func (s Site) staffRoutes(mux *http.ServeMux, secured func(http.Handler) http.Handler) {
	if s.console.Team == nil {
		return
	}
	mux.Handle("GET "+staffPath, secured(s.permittedAny(staffPermissions, http.HandlerFunc(s.staffPage))))
	for action, permission := range staffActions {
		path := staffPath + "/{account}/" + action
		mux.Handle("GET "+path, secured(s.permitted(permission, s.staffAction(action, false))))
		mux.Handle("POST "+path, secured(s.permitted(permission, s.staffAction(action, true))))
	}
}

// permittedAny is permitted for a page any of permissions opens.
func (s Site) permittedAny(permissions []staff.Permission, next http.Handler) http.Handler {
	return http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
		if !s.mayOpenAny(r, permissions) {
			http.NotFound(w, r)
			return
		}
		next.ServeHTTP(w, r)
	})
}

// mayOpenAny reports whether the request's staff member may open a console
// page that any of permissions opens, across the platform.
func (s Site) mayOpenAny(r *http.Request, permissions []staff.Permission) bool {
	return s.allowsIn(r, staff.ConsoleView, staff.Platform) &&
		slices.ContainsFunc(permissions, func(p staff.Permission) bool { return s.allowsIn(r, p, staff.Platform) })
}

// colleagueView is a staff member as the staff list shows them to the
// request's staff member, with the changes they may make to them.
func (s Site) colleagueView(r *http.Request, c staff.Colleague, reset, deactivate bool) web.Colleague {
	session, _ := SessionFrom(r.Context())
	view := web.Colleague{ID: c.ID, Name: c.Name, Email: c.Email, Owner: c.Owner, Self: c.ID == session.Account.ID,
		Deactivated: c.DeactivatedAt != nil}
	for _, method := range c.Factors {
		view.Factors = append(view.Factors, "console.staff.factor."+string(method))
	}
	for _, h := range c.Roles {
		view.Roles = append(view.Roles, web.HeldRole{Name: h.Name, Owner: h.Owner, Scope: h.Marketplace, Where: h.MarketplaceName})
	}
	changeable := !view.Self && !view.Owner && !view.Deactivated
	view.Reset, view.Deactivate = changeable && reset, changeable && deactivate
	return view
}

func (s Site) staffPage(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
	colleagues, err := s.console.Team.List(r.Context())
	if err != nil {
		s.failed(w, r, "the staff could not be read", err)
		return
	}
	reset, deactivate := s.allows(r, staff.StaffResetFactor), s.allows(r, staff.StaffDeactivate)
	view := web.StaffList{Done: staffDone[r.URL.Query().Get("done")]}
	for _, c := range colleagues {
		view.Members = append(view.Members, s.colleagueView(r, c, reset, deactivate))
	}
	render(w, r, web.StaffPage(s.page(r, staffPath), view))
}

// staffRefusal is how a page says a change to a staff member was refused:
// the key of the message and the status; false for an error that is not a
// refusal the page says.
func staffRefusal(err error) (string, bool) {
	switch {
	case errors.Is(err, staff.ErrOwnAccount):
		return "console.staff.error.own", true
	case errors.Is(err, staff.ErrOwnerProtected):
		return "console.staff.error.owner", true
	case errors.Is(err, identity.ErrDeactivated):
		return "console.staff.error.deactivated", true
	}
	return "", false
}

// refusalOf is the refusal the page shows before a change is asked for:
// the same the service would answer, so a change that cannot be made is not
// offered.
func refusalOf(member web.Colleague) error {
	switch {
	case member.Self:
		return staff.ErrOwnAccount
	case member.Owner:
		return staff.ErrOwnerProtected
	case member.Deactivated:
		return identity.ErrDeactivated
	}
	return nil
}

// staffAction serves the confirmation of action on the staff member the
// path names, and, when post, makes it: each asks for the second factor
// again first, back to the confirmation (D10). A member who is not there is
// "not found"; one the change cannot be made to is told why, with no form.
func (s Site) staffAction(action string, post bool) http.HandlerFunc {
	return func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
		id := r.PathValue("account")
		path := staffPath + "/" + id + "/" + action
		member, err := s.console.Team.Colleague(r.Context(), id)
		if errors.Is(err, staff.ErrNotStaff) {
			http.NotFound(w, r)
			return
		}
		if err != nil {
			s.failed(w, r, "a staff member could not be read", err)
			return
		}
		view := web.StaffAction{Member: s.colleagueView(r, member, true, true), Action: action}
		if key, refused := staffRefusal(refusalOf(view.Member)); refused {
			view.Form.Error = key
			renderStatus(w, r, http.StatusConflict, web.StaffActionPage(s.page(r, path), view))
			return
		}
		if s.stepUpFirst(w, r, path) {
			return
		}
		if !post {
			render(w, r, web.StaffActionPage(s.page(r, path), view))
			return
		}
		change, done := s.console.Team.ResetFactor, "reset"
		if action == "deactivate" {
			change, done = s.console.Team.Deactivate, "deactivated"
		}
		err = change(r.Context(), actor(r), visit(r), id)
		if key, refused := staffRefusal(err); refused {
			view.Form.Error = key
			renderStatus(w, r, http.StatusConflict, web.StaffActionPage(s.page(r, path), view))
			return
		}
		switch {
		case errors.Is(err, identity.ErrStaffUnknown), errors.Is(err, staff.ErrNotAllowed):
			http.NotFound(w, r)
		case err != nil:
			s.failed(w, r, "a staff member could not be changed", err)
		default:
			http.Redirect(w, r, "/"+i18n.FromContext(r.Context())+staffPath+"?done="+done, http.StatusSeeOther)
		}
	}
}
```

```bash
git add -A cmd/marketplace internal/platform/httpx web
git commit -F - <<'EOF'
The staff list: each member's roles and second factor, a factor reset and a deactivation, confirmed

The console's staff list (F15 spec, Flows "Staff list"): every staff member
with the roles they hold and where, the kinds of second factor they have,
the owner and the reader marked, and a deactivated member marked apart. It
opens to whoever may invite, deactivate or reset factors across the
platform, and is "not found" to anybody else (D8); the menu gains its entry
for them.

Each member offers the changes the reader's roles allow, on whoever they
can be made to: resetting their second factor and deactivating them, each
confirmed on a page of its own that says what the change does, after the
second factor is asked again (D10). A change nobody may make — to one's own
account, to the owner, to a deactivated member — is said on that page, in
both languages, and not offered; one posted anyway is refused the same way.
A reset colleague is signed out, and adds a new factor only through the
link the reset mails; a deactivated one is answered as a wrong password is.
EOF
```
