Create `e2e/test_console_story.py`:

```python
"""F15 from end to end, against a fresh database: the whole story in one run.

The owner is created by the first run, adds an app and sees the recovery
codes; makes a role that opens the console and nothing more; invites a
colleague with it for one marketplace. The colleague accepts the invitation
in a browser of their own, adds an app, signs in again with it and sees only
what the role permits — the version endpoint included. The owner resets the
colleague's second factor: the colleague's password alone then opens
nothing, and the link the reset mails, with the password, leads to a new
app. The owner deactivates the colleague last. The run leaves screenshots of
the console in both languages, and the audit records it produced are
asserted one by one: each action, who did it, to what, in one unbroken
chain of the platform's, with no personal data in its clear columns
(docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-26-f15-console-design.md, Testing).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
import re

import psycopg
import pytest
from playwright.sync_api import expect, sync_playwright

from accounts import PASSWORD, SIGNED_IN, SUBMIT, sign_in, wait_for_mail
from console import CONSOLE_HOST, OWNER, OWNER_NAME, add_app, first_run, health_version
from factors import CODE_SUBMIT, SIGN_OUT, next_totp

# As web/locales words them.
CREATED = {"pt-BR": "O papel foi criado.", "en-US": "The role was created."}
INVITED = {"pt-BR": "O convite foi enviado.", "en-US": "The invitation was sent."}
RESET = {"pt-BR": "O segundo fator foi redefinido.", "en-US": "The second factor was reset."}
DEACTIVATED = {"pt-BR": "O membro da equipe foi desativado.", "en-US": "The staff member was deactivated."}
USE_THE_LINK = {
    "pt-BR": "Use o link do e-mail que você recebeu",
    "en-US": "Use the link in the e-mail you received",
}
REFUSED = {
    "pt-BR": "O e-mail ou a senha não conferem.",
    "en-US": "The e-mail or the password is not right.",
}
IN_STORE = {"pt-BR": "Leitura — em: Loja Um", "en-US": "Leitura — in: Loja Um"}

# What the run is audited as, in order: the action, the kind of actor and
# who, and the kind of subject and which, with "owner", "colleague", "role"
# and "invitation" standing for the identifiers the run made. Every record
# is the platform's. A staff account's creation runs its hook first, so the
# owner's and the invitation's records come before the account's own. The
# refused sign-ins name no actor: whoever typed the password is not known to
# be the account's owner (internal/identity).
STORY = [
    # The first run creates the owner and signs them in; they add an app.
    ("staff.owner_created", "staff", "owner", "account", "owner"),
    ("identity.staff_created", "staff", "owner", "account", "owner"),
    ("identity.signin", "staff", "owner", "account", "owner"),
    ("identity.second_factor_added", "staff", "owner", "account", "owner"),
    # A role made, after the second factor is asked again.
    ("identity.stepped_up", "staff", "owner", "account", "owner"),
    ("staff.role_created", "staff", "owner", "role", "role"),
    # The invitation, sent and accepted; the colleague adds an app.
    ("staff.invitation_sent", "staff", "owner", "invitation", "invitation"),
    ("staff.invitation_accepted", "staff", "colleague", "account", "colleague"),
    ("identity.staff_created", "staff", "colleague", "account", "colleague"),
    ("identity.signin", "staff", "colleague", "account", "colleague"),
    ("identity.second_factor_added", "staff", "colleague", "account", "colleague"),
    # The colleague signs out and in again, with the app.
    ("identity.signout", "staff", "colleague", "account", "colleague"),
    ("identity.signin", "staff", "colleague", "account", "colleague"),
    # The reset, the password alone refused, and the mailed link used.
    ("staff.factor_reset", "staff", "owner", "account", "colleague"),
    ("identity.signin_failed", "system", None, "account", "colleague"),
    ("identity.factor_reset_link_used", "staff", "colleague", "account", "colleague"),
    ("identity.signin", "staff", "colleague", "account", "colleague"),
    ("identity.second_factor_added", "staff", "colleague", "account", "colleague"),
    # The deactivation, and the password answered as a wrong one.
    ("staff.deactivated", "staff", "owner", "account", "colleague"),
    ("identity.signin_failed", "system", None, "account", "colleague"),
]


def audit_records(console) -> list[dict]:
    """Every record of the audit log, oldest first, as its clear columns say
    it (internal/platform/audit): what is sealed stays sealed."""
    with psycopg.connect(console.database.owner_url) as conn:
        cursor = conn.execute(
            "SELECT marketplace_id::text AS marketplace, sequence, actor_id::text AS actor, actor_kind,"
            "       action, subject_kind, subject_id, previous_hash, hash, actor_secret, subject_secret"
            "  FROM audit_log ORDER BY id"
        )
        names = [column.name for column in cursor.description]
        return [dict(zip(names, row)) for row in cursor.fetchall()]


def identifiers(console, colleague_address: str) -> dict[str, str]:
    """The identifiers the run made, by the names STORY gives them."""
    with psycopg.connect(console.database.owner_url) as conn:
        def one(statement, *args):
            return conn.execute(statement, args).fetchone()[0]
        return {
            one("SELECT id::text FROM account WHERE email_normalised = %s", OWNER): "owner",
            one("SELECT id::text FROM account WHERE email_normalised = %s", colleague_address): "colleague",
            one("SELECT id::text FROM role WHERE name = 'Leitura'"): "role",
            one("SELECT id::text FROM staff_invitation WHERE email_normalised = %s", colleague_address): "invitation",
        }


@pytest.mark.local_process
@pytest.mark.parametrize("language", ["pt-BR", "en-US"])
def test_the_console_from_its_first_run_to_a_colleagues_offboarding(run_console, screenshots, language):
    console = run_console()
    version = health_version(console)
    colleague_address = f"colega-historia-{language.lower()}@example.test"
    home = console.url(CONSOLE_HOST, f"/{language}/")

    def shot(page, name):
        page.screenshot(path=screenshots / f"f15-story-{name}-{language}.png", full_page=True)

    with sync_playwright() as p:
        browser = p.chromium.launch()

        # The first run: the owner, an app, the recovery codes, the console.
        owner = browser.new_page()
        first_run(owner, console, language)
        expect(owner).to_have_url(re.compile(rf"/{language}/enrol$"))
        owner_key = add_app(owner)
        shot(owner, "recovery-codes")
        owner.goto(home)
        expect(owner.locator(SIGNED_IN)).to_contain_text(OWNER_NAME)
        expect(owner.locator("footer")).to_contain_text(version)
        shot(owner, "home")
        owner.goto(console.url(CONSOLE_HOST, "/console/version"))
        assert json.loads(owner.locator("body").inner_text()) == {"version": version}

        # A role that opens the console's home and nothing more.
        owner.goto(console.url(CONSOLE_HOST, f"/{language}/roles/new"))
        owner.fill("main form input[name=code]", next_totp(owner_key))
        owner.click(CODE_SUBMIT)
        owner.fill("main form input[name=name]", "Leitura")
        owner.check("input[name=permission][value='console.view']")
        owner.click(SUBMIT)
        expect(owner.locator("[role=status]")).to_have_text(CREATED[language])
        shot(owner, "roles")

        # The colleague, invited with it for one marketplace.
        owner.goto(console.url(CONSOLE_HOST, f"/{language}/staff/invite"))
        owner.fill("main form input[name=name]", "Colega")
        owner.fill("main form input[name=email]", colleague_address)
        role = owner.locator("input[name=grant]").get_attribute("value")
        owner.check(f"input[name=grant][value='{role}']")
        owner.select_option(f"select[name='scope-{role}']", label="Loja Um")
        owner.click(SUBMIT)
        expect(owner.locator("[role=status]")).to_have_text(INVITED[language])

        # The invitation accepted in the colleague's own browser, and an app.
        colleague = browser.new_context().new_page()
        colleague.goto(wait_for_mail(console.mailbox, "staff-invitation", colleague_address)["variables"]["Link"])
        colleague.fill("main form input[name=password]", PASSWORD)
        colleague.click(SUBMIT)
        expect(colleague).to_have_url(re.compile(rf"/{language}/enrol$"))
        colleague_key = add_app(colleague)

        # Signed in again with the app, the colleague sees what the role
        # permits and not a page more: not the version either.
        colleague.goto(home)
        colleague.click(SIGN_OUT)
        sign_in(colleague, console, colleague_address, host=CONSOLE_HOST, language=language)
        colleague.fill("main form input[name=code]", next_totp(colleague_key))
        colleague.click(CODE_SUBMIT)
        expect(colleague).to_have_url(re.compile(rf"/{language}/$"))
        expect(colleague.locator("nav.menu a")).to_have_count(3)
        expect(colleague.locator("nav.menu a[href$='/roles'], nav.menu a[href$='/staff']")).to_have_count(0)
        shot(colleague, "colleague-home")
        for path in (f"/{language}/roles", f"/{language}/staff", f"/{language}/staff/invite", "/console/version"):
            answer = colleague.goto(console.url(CONSOLE_HOST, path))
            assert answer.status == 404, (path, answer.status)

        # The owner's staff list shows the colleague's role where, and app.
        owner.goto(console.url(CONSOLE_HOST, f"/{language}/staff"))
        member = owner.locator("ul.staff > li", has_text=colleague_address)
        expect(member).to_contain_text(IN_STORE[language])
        shot(owner, "staff")

        # A reset: the password alone opens nothing; the mailed link does.
        member.locator("a[href$='/reset']").click()
        owner.click(SUBMIT)
        expect(owner.locator("[role=status]")).to_have_text(RESET[language])
        colleague.goto(home)
        expect(colleague).to_have_url(re.compile(r"/signin$"))
        sign_in(colleague, console, colleague_address, host=CONSOLE_HOST, language=language)
        expect(colleague.locator("[role=alert]")).to_contain_text(USE_THE_LINK[language])
        colleague.goto(wait_for_mail(console.mailbox, "second-factor-reset", colleague_address)["variables"]["Link"])
        shot(colleague, "new-factor")
        colleague.fill("main form input[name=password]", PASSWORD)
        colleague.click(SUBMIT)
        expect(colleague).to_have_url(re.compile(rf"/{language}/enrol$"))
        add_app(colleague)
        colleague.goto(home)
        expect(colleague.locator(SIGNED_IN)).to_contain_text("Colega")

        # The deactivation: signed out, and the password refused.
        owner.goto(console.url(CONSOLE_HOST, f"/{language}/staff"))
        owner.locator("ul.staff > li", has_text=colleague_address).locator("a[href$='/deactivate']").click()
        owner.click(SUBMIT)
        expect(owner.locator("[role=status]")).to_have_text(DEACTIVATED[language])
        shot(owner, "staff-after")
        colleague.goto(home)
        expect(colleague).to_have_url(re.compile(r"/signin$"))
        sign_in(colleague, console, colleague_address, host=CONSOLE_HOST, language=language)
        expect(colleague.locator("[role=alert]")).to_have_text(REFUSED[language])
        browser.close()

    # Every record is the platform's, in one chain: the seed's two
    # marketplaces first, made by the system, then the story.
    records = audit_records(console)
    assert all(r["marketplace"] is None for r in records)
    seeded, story = records[:2], records[2:]
    assert [(r["action"], r["actor_kind"]) for r in seeded] == [("marketplace.created", "system")] * 2, seeded
    names = identifiers(console, colleague_address)
    told = [(r["action"], r["actor_kind"], names.get(r["actor"], r["actor"]), r["subject_kind"],
             names.get(r["subject_id"], r["subject_id"])) for r in story]
    assert told == STORY

    # One chain, unbroken: each record names the one before it.
    assert [r["sequence"] for r in records] == list(range(1, len(records) + 1))
    for before, after in zip(records, records[1:]):
        assert after["previous_hash"] == before["hash"], (before["action"], after["action"])

    # Nothing personal in clear: no name or address in any clear column, and
    # none in a sealed one's bytes either.
    for record in story:
        clear = " ".join(str(record[c]) for c in ("actor", "actor_kind", "action", "subject_kind", "subject_id"))
        assert "@" not in clear and "Colega" not in clear and OWNER_NAME not in clear, clear
        for sealed in (record["actor_secret"], record["subject_secret"]):
            assert colleague_address.encode() not in bytes(sealed or b""), record["action"]
            assert OWNER.encode() not in bytes(sealed or b""), record["action"]
```

## Task 21: F15 delivered: the roadmap ticked, the spec's status, and the console's host check left as a recorded follow-up

The console's host stays out of the deployment's host check, on purpose: its Cloudflare
record is the owner's first manual step, and every deployment reaches each host it reads from
`lab.tfvars` and fails when one does not answer, so a check added now would turn every deployment red
until the record exists. `docs/infrastructure.md` records the one rule the workflow's host list gains
and when to add it (the roadmap's follow-up and the spec's risk already point there). Then the roadmap's
F15 is ticked with the pull requests that delivered it, and the spec's status records the delivery.

**Not yet in the deployment's host check.** Every deployment reaches the

`hosts` — and fails when one does not answer as the new build
(`.github/workflows/deploy.yml`; `e2e/test_hosts.py`). The console's host is
left out on purpose while its record is a manual step: a check that fails
until the owner creates the record would turn every deployment red, for a
wait everybody expects. It is a follow-up, not an omission
(`docs/roadmap.md`, F15): once `getent hosts` above answers and
`https://console.marketplace.lab.aleogr.dev/health` answers with the
deployed build, the step "Read the region, the build identifier and the
hosts" gains one rule in its `awk` program, reading `console_host` as it
reads `platform_host`:

```
/^[[:space:]]*console_host[[:space:]]*=/ {
  if (match($0, /"[^"]+"/)) print substr($0, RSTART + 1, RLENGTH - 2)
}
```

The console serves `/health` like every host, so the check needs nothing
else. The first deployment after that change proves the console's host
answers as the same build as the others.

## What the plan adds to the spec, and why

None of these changes a decision; each is how the plan builds one. They are listed so that nobody
reading the spec is surprised by them.

- **`bootstrap.singleton`** (migration 00017): a `boolean PRIMARY KEY CHECK (singleton)` column, which is
  how "at most one row" is said to the database; two first runs at once serialise on it and the second
  is `ErrSetupClosed`.
- **`user_role.owner` and `user_role.account_kind`** (migration 00019), each the second half of a
  composite foreign key: `(role_id, owner)` to `role (id, owner)` makes an assignment say truthfully
  whether it is the owner's, so "one assignment of the owner role, for the whole platform" is a partial
  unique index and a check; `(account_id, account_kind)` to `account (id, kind)` with `account_kind =
  'staff'` makes "a role is held by staff only" a constraint. Triggers are kept for what constraints
  cannot say (the owner role's name, permissions and assignment unchanged). Role names are unique
  case-insensitively (`role_name_is_unique`), so nobody makes a role called "Owner".
- **`staff_invitation.email_normalised`** and **`staff_invitation_role.owner`** (migration 00023): the
  address as F13 compares it, for the one open invitation per address; and the same `(role_id, owner)`
  key as `user_role`, always false, so the owner role cannot be offered.
- **`account_only_staff_are_offboarded`** (migration 00021): `deactivated_at` and `factor_reset_at` stay
  null on buyers' accounts.
- **The permission table is read-only to the application role** (`db.grant` revokes the rest, as it does
  the audit log's), and the synchronisation runs in `cmd/marketplace`'s `migrate` rather than in
  `db.Migrate`, which must not import a domain package.
- **The session sweep names its scope** (`= $4` or `IS NULL`): `IS NOT DISTINCT FROM`, which D4 fixes for
  the policies, cannot use `session_by_marketplace`.
- **Staff accounts are created with their address verified**: whoever holds the bootstrap token or the
  invitation's link vouches for it; there is no confirmation mail for staff.
- **The step-up action `staff`** (`identity.ActionStaff`): one F14 action for every change to the
  platform's staff (D10), asked always and never answered by an e-mail code for staff. The form pages ask
  before they are shown, so a post is not lost.
- **The limits' numbers:** `setup` and `invitation` allow 10 posts per hour per client address; the reset
  link's post counts under the sign-in's limit, since it is a sign-in.
- **The console also mounts the staff member's own security and password pages** (F14's, in the
  console's shell) and no e-mail-code route; `/console/version` answers `{"version": "…"}` with
  `Cache-Control: no-store`, outside the language prefix. The console's name (`console.title`, in the
  request's language) is the authenticator app's issuer, the key's relying-party name and the mails'
  sender name, as a marketplace's name is on its host.
- **Invalid links answer 410 Gone**, with one sentence, for an invitation or a reset link that is
  unknown, used, replaced or expired, as F13's `/verify` does.
- **A deactivated account's sign-in is audited** `identity.signin_failed` with `{"reason": "deactivated"}`,
  after the same argon2 work as a wrong password; the answer is the same.
- **Assigning another role to the owner is allowed** (harmless); PR 2's end-to-end test uses it, since
  the owner is then the only staff member.
- **A test database per first run** (`dbtest.Fresh`, `another()` in the end-to-end suite), since the first
  run happens once per database.

**Placeholders.** None: every code step holds the whole code, and every command its expected result.
`#PRS` in Task 21 stands for the four pull-request numbers, which exist only once GitHub assigns them;
the step says where they go.

**Type consistency.** The names each task produces are listed in its Interfaces block and are the
ones later tasks use; the transcription described at the top compiled and passed every check at the
end of each pull request, which is what an inconsistency would have broken.
